## Day 3  (situations: sick)

**daniel plans the day** (stress 1, tired 3, mood 1, health 1): 05:45 Wake feeling chilled and congested, use the bathroom, wash my hands and face, and drink water slowly. (bathroom); 06:10 Sit on the closed toilet lid and call the service office to report that I cannot work today, then send the necessary cancellation messages from my phone. (bathroom); 06:30 Take my allergy tablets and a dose of cold medicine with water, then move slowly to the bedroom and lie down. (main_bedroom); 07:00 Sleep or doze under the quilt while Mara gets ready for her day. (main_bedroom); 08:00 Wake for water and a little banana, then leave the mug and peel on the bedside surface rather than attempting a proper breakfast. (main_bedroom); 08:20 Rest quietly in bed, answering Mara briefly when she checks on me and keeping tissues nearby. (main_bedroom); 10:30 Get up carefully, wash my hands, refill my water bottle, and use the bathroom. (bathroom); 10:45 Lie on the sofa with the throw blanket, listen to quiet television for a while, and doze when the congestion makes reading impossible. (living); 12:30 Eat a few spoonfuls of yogurt and drink water, then put the bowl and spoon in the kitchen sink. (kitchen); 12:50 Return to the sofa and sleep, keeping my phone within reach in case the office needs clarification. (living); 15:00 Check in with Mara by text, drink more water, and take my temperature. (living); 15:15 Rest in bed again, mostly asleep, with tissues and water beside me. (main_bedroom); 17:30 Wake, wash my hands, and sit quietly while Mara brings or sets aside something plain for me; eat only what I can manage. (kitchen); 18:00 Rest on the sofa with the television low, taking small drinks and speaking only when needed. (living); 19:30 Take more cold medicine only if the label interval allows, drink water, and brush my teeth slowly. (bathroom); 19:45 Change into sleep clothes and put the worn evening clothes in the laundry hamper. (main_bedroom); 20:00 Sleep, waking briefly for water or tissues if necessary. (main_bedroom)
> *daniel thinks:* I woke up with a sudden heavy cold: sore throat, chills, and very little energy. I am not fit to drive between calls, so I will notify the office and customers, then keep the day deliberately small. Mara can handle her own work and errands; I will accept practical help without turning her into a supervisor. I will focus on water, quiet rest, and basic hygiene, with only enough checking in to reassure her.

## Day 4  (situations: sick)

**mara plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 06:15 Wake, wash up, take my prescription if due, and get dressed for work. I carry my reading glasses from the living-room side table upstairs and set them on the left nightstand when I am finished with them. (main_bedroom); 06:30 Make coffee and toast, then check the weather and transit alerts on my phone at the kitchen island. I use the blue ceramic mug and leave it at the island center when I finish. (kitchen); 07:15 Finish getting ready, pack my work laptop into its padded backpack, and gather my wallet, keys, transit badge, and headphones. I put the keys in the maple console's left drawer before leaving. (entry); 07:45 Commute to the municipal office with my work backpack and transit badge. (out_of_house); 08:30 Work at the municipal office, attend meetings, and review street-project maps and the revised traffic-calming proposal. I make notes about the Thursday changes and keep the deadline visible in my planner. (out_of_house); 12:30 Take a quiet lunch break and check my phone for any message from home, without repeatedly interrupting Daniel. (out_of_house); 13:00 Continue meetings, map review, and revisions to the traffic-calming proposal. I organize the points that still need checking so I can finish the most important work from home this evening. (out_of_house); 17:00 Commute home with my work backpack, keeping the revised proposal notes together for the evening work session. (out_of_house); 17:45 Put my work things in bedroom two, change into leggings and a cardigan, check quietly on Daniel, and make ginger tea. I carry the tea mug to the right side of the living-room coffee table and leave it there for him. (living); 18:15 Work from the bedroom-two desk on the neighborhood traffic-calming proposal, using my notes and map folder. I keep the door open enough to hear if Daniel needs me and take one brief pause to check that he is comfortable. (bedroom_two); 19:15 Bring a simple reheated dinner to the dining table and eat quietly with Daniel, leaving room for him to rest rather than expecting conversation. I use a bowl, spoon, glass, and napkin, then carry my things back toward the kitchen when I finish. (dining); 20:00 Wash my dinner things and do a small reset of the kitchen counter and entry. I check the tea on the coffee table and replace it with fresh ginger tea if it has gone cold. (kitchen); 20:20 Read a few chapters of my library book with tea on the living-room sofa, keeping the television off or very quiet. I glance over to check on Daniel but do not interrupt his rest. (living); 21:15 Put away the tea things, wash up, and prepare for bed. I put my phone on its charger, set tomorrow's work reminder, and leave my reading glasses on the living-room side table if I am still carrying them. (bathroom); 21:45 Get into bed, read a few quiet pages, and turn out the light. (main_bedroom)
> *mara thinks:* Daniel is still unwell on the second day, so I will keep the morning routine and workday mostly intact but make the evening gentler and more organized around his recovery. Thursday's traffic-plan deadline adds some background stress, though I can handle the proposal from home and avoid expecting much conversation at dinner.
**daniel plans the day** (stress 1, tired 3, mood 1, health 1) [off-routine day]: 06:15 Wake congested and chilled, sit up slowly, drink water, and use the bathroom. Wash my hands and face before returning to the bedroom. (bathroom); 06:35 Take my allergy tablets and cold medicine with water if the label interval allows, then lie back down under the quilt. (main_bedroom); 07:00 Sleep or doze in bed, keeping my phone and water within reach. Answer Mara briefly if she checks in. (main_bedroom); 09:30 Eat a little yogurt and banana at the bedside, taking small drinks between bites. Leave the bowl and spoon on the nightstand rather than trying to clean up immediately. (main_bedroom); 09:50 Rest quietly in bed with the television off, using tissues as needed and sending Mara a short message that I have eaten a little. (main_bedroom); 11:30 Get up carefully, refill my water bottle at the kitchen sink, and wash my hands. Put the used bowl and spoon in the sink. (kitchen); 11:50 Lie on the sofa with the throw blanket and listen to quiet television, dozing on and off. (living); 13:30 Check my temperature and drink more water. Take another dose of cold medicine only if the label interval has passed. (living); 13:45 Sleep on the sofa, leaving the phone on the coffee table and the water bottle beside me. (living); 15:45 Take a slow shower, brush my teeth, and put on clean sleep clothes. Leave the used clothes in the laundry hamper. (bathroom); 16:10 Return to bed and rest, drinking water when I wake. Keep the room quiet while Mara finishes her workday. (main_bedroom); 18:00 Sit at the dining table and eat a small portion of Mara's prepared food, using a plate and fork. Stop if my stomach objects and drink water slowly. (dining); 18:30 Remain seated at the table for a short, quiet conversation with Mara, then carry my plate and fork to the kitchen sink. (dining); 19:00 Rest on the sofa with low television, leaving the remote and water within reach. Tell Mara I am going to bed early if the fatigue returns. (living); 20:15 Brush my teeth slowly, wash my hands, and take any permitted evening medicine with water. (bathroom); 20:30 Sleep in bed, keeping water, tissues, and my phone beside me for brief nighttime wake-ups. (main_bedroom)
> *daniel thinks:* I am still clearly sick, though the worst congestion seems a little less intense than yesterday. ## Day 5  (situations: sick)

**mara plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 06:15 Wake, wash up, take my prescription if due, and get dressed for work. I bring my reading glasses from the living-room side table upstairs and set them on the left nightstand when I am finished with them. (main_bedroom); 06:30 Make coffee and toast, then check the weather and transit alerts on my phone at the kitchen island. I use the blue ceramic mug and leave it at the island center when I finish. (kitchen); 07:15 Finish getting ready, pack my work laptop into its padded backpack, and gather my wallet, keys, transit badge, and headphones. I put the keys in the maple console's left drawer before leaving. (entry); 07:45 Commute to the municipal office. (out_of_house); 08:30 Work at the municipal office on transportation planning, meetings, and reviewing street-project maps. I keep my phone available in case Daniel needs me, but avoid hovering over it during meetings. (out_of_house); 17:00 Commute home from the municipal office and stop for any necessary basic food on the way if the house does not have an easy dinner option. (out_of_house); 17:45 Come in, put my work backpack in bedroom two, change into leggings and a cardigan, and check quietly on Daniel without pressing him to talk. (bedroom_two); 18:05 Make ginger tea and carry it to the right side of the living-room coffee table. I check whether Daniel needs water or medicine, then give him space rather than asking repeated questions. (kitchen); 18:25 Prepare a simple dinner for two, using a saucepan and pantry ingredients rather than expecting Daniel to cook. I set the dining table plainly and keep portions modest in case he has little appetite. (kitchen); 19:00 Eat dinner with Daniel at the dining table, keeping conversation light and allowing him to leave early if he feels worse. I carry my bowl, spoon, glass, and napkin back toward the kitchen when finished. (dining); 19:45 Wash the dinner things and do a small reset of the kitchen counter and entry. I check the tea on the coffee table and replace it with fresh ginger tea if it has gone cold. (kitchen); 20:10 Read my library book on the living-room sofa with tea nearby, keeping the television off or very quiet. I glance over periodically to check on Daniel without interrupting his rest. (living); 21:00 Put away the tea things, wash up, and prepare for bed. I put my phone on its charger and set a quiet reminder to check Daniel once more before sleep. (main_bedroom); 21:30 Get into bed, read a few quiet pages, and turn out the light. (main_bedroom)
> *mara thinks:* Daniel is still ill on day three, so I will keep my normal workday but make the morning and evening quieter and more attentive. Since he usually cooks on Fridays and may not feel up to it, I will plan a simple dinner I can prepare without expecting conversation or help. I will check on him after work, make ginger tea, and keep the evening low-key.

## Day 6  (situations: relative_visit)

**mara plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 07:30 Wake, wash up, take my prescription if due, and get dressed in comfortable clothes for laundry and errands. (main_bedroom); 08:00 Change the bed linens, start and transfer laundry, and fold the dry clothes at the laundry counter. I set aside a clean sheet set and fresh towels for the relative, then put the folded clothes away. (laundry); 10:00 Make coffee and toast, check the weather and transit alerts on my phone, and do a quick guest reset of the entry, living room, and bathroom. I leave the blue ceramic mug at the kitchen island center when finished. (kitchen); 10:30 Go to the farmers market and run the usual errands, carrying my tote bag and grocery list. I transfer any useful list items to my phone before shopping. (out_of_house); 12:00 Return home, put the market items away, set out water and fruit, and check the guest room and bathroom once more. I leave the mail stack undisturbed on the dining table rather than sorting it today. (kitchen); 12:30 Welcome the relative and have a light lunch at the dining table. I keep portions and conversation easy, redirecting gently if opinions become too pointed. (dining); 13:15 Spend some time talking with the relative in the living room, then excuse myself for a short practical reset: clear dishes, refresh the coffee table, and make space for everyone to sit comfortably. (living); 14:00 Take a quiet tea break at the kitchen island while the relative has independent time. I check messages and make a brief note in my planner about any errands or household details that can wait. (kitchen); 14:30 Rejoin the relative for an easy afternoon visit, offering tea or water and showing them where to settle their things. I keep the conversation kind and do not press Daniel to participate continuously. (living); 16:00 Do a modest kitchen and entry reset, wash the afternoon dishes, and prepare ingredients for a simple dinner. I leave any mail sorting and nonessential tidying for another day. (kitchen); 16:30 Spend a quieter stretch with the relative, offering the living room and guest room as comfortable places to read or rest. I read a few pages of my library book if conversation naturally pauses. (living); 18:00 Prepare and serve a simple shared dinner at the dining table, using market food and pantry ingredients. I keep the meal uncomplicated and apologize lightly for not making a more elaborate plan. (kitchen); 19:00 Eat dinner with the relative and Daniel if he joins, keeping the conversation light. I carry the used dishes toward the kitchen afterward and leave space for anyone who wants to finish early. (dining); 19:45 Wash the dinner things, wipe the kitchen counters, and put fresh water and a small snack where the relative can find them. I make a quick visual reset of the entry and sofa area. (kitchen); 20:15 Have a low-key evening with the relative in the living room, keeping the television quiet or off. I make tea as needed and take a short quiet pause in the kitchen if I feel conversation becoming too much. (living); 21:30 See that the guest has what they need for the night, put away the tea things, and make one final small reset of the living room. I avoid starting any larger organizing task. (living); 22:00 Wash up, change into pajamas, put my phone on its charger, and set my reading glasses on the left nightstand. I make a quiet note in my planner for tomorrow's household follow-up. (main_bedroom); 22:30 Read a few quiet pages in bed and turn out the light. (main_bedroom)
> *mara thinks:* I wake reasonably rested and keep the morning structure where I can: laundry first, then the market. Because the relative's visit may make the house feel crowded, I will make the guest room and bathroom comfortable before settling into hosting. I will keep conversation warm but leave myself small quiet intervals, and avoid assuming Daniel will cook or entertain continuously.
**daniel plans the day** (stress 1, tired 3, mood 1, health 1): 07:30 Wake slowly, sit up, drink from the water bottle, and use the bathroom. Wash my hands and face before returning to the bedroom. (main_bedroom); 08:20 Eat a small bedside breakfast of yogurt and banana, taking slow drinks between bites. Carry the empty bowl and spoon to the kitchen sink afterward, then return to bed. (main_bedroom); 09:00 Rest in the dim bedroom, dozing or reading a little while Mara handles her laundry routine. Answer briefly if she checks on me. (main_bedroom); 10:00 Get up carefully, refill the water bottle at the kitchen sink, wash my hands, and sit at the kitchen counter for a few minutes. (kitchen); 10:20 Welcome the visiting relative warmly but briefly, staying seated and explaining that I am recovering from a cold and may need quiet breaks. (living); 10:45 Lie down on the sofa with the throw blanket while the relative settles in. Keep conversation limited and use the remote for quiet television if needed. (living); 11:30 Check my temperature, drink water, and send Mara a short message that I am resting and have eaten. Remain on the sofa rather than attempting chores. (living); 12:00 Have a small additional snack if hungry, carrying a bowl and spoon from the kitchen to the living room and setting them on the coffee table. Stop if nausea returns. (living); 12:30 Rest quietly on the sofa while Mara is out at the market and errands. If the relative wants conversation, respond pleasantly for a few minutes, then say I need to close my eyes. (living); 13:00 Doze on the sofa with the television off, keeping the phone and water bottle beside me. Leave any opinions or practical discussions for another day. (living); 14:00 Wake, wash my hands and face at the bathroom sink, and check whether another medicine dose is permitted. Take it with water only if the label interval has passed. (bathroom); 14:20 Sit or recline in the living room while the relative visits. Offer brief conversation and affection, then use the quiet time when conversation pauses to rest. (living); 15:30 Carry the water bottle to the kitchen, refill it, and return to the sofa. Put any used bowl or spoon in the sink, leaving the rest of the cleanup for Mara. (kitchen); 16:00 Rest on the sofa with low television or silence. Keep conversation minimal and tell Mara if I need to lie down or if the fever rises. (living); 17:30 Eat a small portion of whatever Mara prepares, sitting at the dining table with water. Stop eating if nausea returns and leave the plate and fork at the kitchen sink afterward. (dining); 18:15 Return to the sofa and rest while Mara and the relative manage their own evening conversation. Give short answers when included and take a quiet break when needed. (living); 20:00 Check my temperature, drink water, and take permitted evening medicine. Carry the bottle and thermometer back to the bedroom. (main_bedroom); 20:30 Brush my teeth slowly, wash my hands and face, and change into sleep pants and a sleep shirt. Put the water bottle, tissues, and phone beside the bed. (bathroom); 21:00 Sleep under the quilt, waking only for water, medicine if permitted, or the bathroom. (main_bedroom)

## Day 7  (situations: relative_visit, late_service_call)

**mara plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 07:30 Wake, wash up, take my prescription if due, and dress in comfortable clothes. I check that the relative has towels and water without disturbing them, then carry my phone downstairs. (main_bedroom); 08:15 Make a simple breakfast for myself and set out fruit and water for the relative. I carry my plate and mug to the dining table, leaving the blue ceramic mug at the kitchen island afterward rather than risking it being used for anything else. (kitchen); 08:45 Do a small guest and downstairs reset: clear any obvious dishes, straighten the sofa, and check the bathroom supplies. I avoid starting a larger organizing project. (living); 09:00 Have coffee, work through a crossword, and review the weekly calendar at the dining table. I make a short note about the relative's needs and leave the incoming mail stack undisturbed at the left end of the table. (dining); 10:00 Offer the relative tea or water and explain, lightly, where breakfast things are. I carry the tea to the living room if wanted, then give them room rather than filling the silence. (living); 10:30 While Daniel handles his own late breakfast and kitchen cleanup, I take a quiet household-support interval: refresh the guest room and bathroom, replace a towel if needed, and put stray items back in their usual places. (bathroom); 11:15 Sit with the relative for an easy conversation or shared reading, keeping the topic neutral when opinions become pointed. I bring my library book so I can read if the conversation pauses naturally. (living); 12:15 Prepare and serve a light lunch at the dining table using leftovers and fruit. I keep portions uncomplicated and carry used dishes to the kitchen when we finish. (kitchen); 13:00 Wash the lunch dishes and wipe the kitchen counters, then place a small snack and fresh water where the relative can reach them. I leave Daniel alone if he is mentally occupied with work. (kitchen); 13:30 Take a brief quiet pause in the bedroom with my phone and reading glasses, checking messages and making no new commitments. I listen for whether the relative needs anything, but do not hover. (main_bedroom); 14:00 Give the relative a comfortable independent stretch while I sort a small amount of household paperwork at the dining table. I keep the mail stack intact and only note urgent follow-up items in my planner. (dining); 15:00 Rejoin the relative in the living room with tea or water, then make space for Daniel to have a short quiet interval if he comes in distracted and does not want to talk. I do not ask him to explain the problem twice. (living); 15:45 Wash the tea things and set out the cutting board, knives, and ingredients for the week's casserole. I check that the relative has a comfortable place to sit or read. (kitchen); 16:00 Prepare vegetables and assemble a casserole for the coming week, following my usual Sunday routine. I keep the conversation optional, put the finished dish into the oven, and leave the counters reasonably clear. (kitchen); 17:00 Serve an easy dinner at the dining table, adding fruit or yogurt for the relative if useful. I keep the meal warm but do not insist that Daniel participate continuously if he is still mentally at work. (dining); 18:00 Clear and wash the dinner things, wipe the kitchen, and put leftovers away. I carry a small glass of water and snack to the relative's usual resting place. (kitchen); 18:45 Spend a low-key evening in the living room with the relative, offering tea and letting the television remain quiet or off. I read a few pages when conversation naturally pauses and give Daniel short stretches without questions. (living); 20:15 Do a fast reset of the entry, sofa, and kitchen counters, then check that the relative has water, towels, and anything needed for the night. > I am still keyed up from the long repair and tired enough that I want to get the story out without turning the whole evening into a debrief. 18:00 daniel say to mara The call was supposed to be a quick intermittent-start repair. The customer said the machine would run sometimes and then suddenly stop, so at first I checked the obvious things—the supply, the controls, the thermal readings, and the connections I could reach. It behaved perfectly while I was testing it, which is always a bad sign for an intermittent fault. I kept cycling it and finally took the rear panel farther apart than I expected. The real problem was hidden behind the panel: a terminal had loosened and started to discolor from heat, so it was making contact until vibration or temperature changed. I had to remove more of the housing, clean and tighten the terminal, replace the damaged connector, and test it through several cycles. That was what made the job run long—the fault was concealed, and the machine would not fail on demand. Then, just as I was packing up, the customer had one more question about whether the repair would hold and wanted me to explain what warning signs to watch for. I understand why they asked, but it added another stretch of testing and explaining. I left it working, but I am still replaying whether I should have checked behind that panel sooner.  _(Share the responsibility I am replaying without demanding a long conversation.)_
